Examples include the Bürgergeld Act, Wohngeld-Plus, the care-support law, the National Security Strategy, the Bundeswehr special fund and Ukraine-support commitments, and the fulfillment of the 2023 family-reunification visa quota. These establish partial end-period conversion, but not durable achievement across the program as a whole. Several measures retained future deadlines or lacked demonstrated outcomes; defense readiness and funding durability remained uncertain, administrative digitalization had missed its earlier target, Afghan admission implementation was severely delayed, transport and oversight failures persisted, and the constitutional budget ruling destabilized planned climate and industrial financing. Despite court bans, police first tried to accommodate the protest, but broke it up when officers came under attack.\nCopy link\nImage: Robert Michael/dpa/picture alliance\nAdvertisement\nSeveral police officers were injured in the eastern German city of Leipzig on Saturday in clashes with\nleft-wing protesters\n.\nLeipzig police spokesman Olaf Hoppe described the situation as \"very dynamic,\" with \"sometimes massive clashes here in the south of Leipzig.\"\nHe said that \"roughly 1,500 people\" turned up to the demonstration, even though several courts had refused to authorize it.\n\"According to our estimates, one-third of them were either inclined towards violence, or were actively seeking violence,\" Hoppe said. \"We observed numerous cases of people intentionally obscuring their faces, which is an offense in and of itself.\"\nAfter clashes overnight, police broke up the demonstration when they were pelted with 'stones, pyrotechnics and other objects'\nImage: Robert Michael/dpa/picture alliance\nHoppe said that as the demonstration grew and as people who appeared to seek violence gathered, organizers and police negotiated for around an hour seeking a way to allow the banned demonstration to go ahead \"in the name of freedom of assembly.\"\nPolice had tried to keep the demonstration, originally envisioned as a march from the south of town to the main current situation, following the protest's dissolution, as \"calm again, but still strained.\"\nAround an hour later, police said that \"roughly 300 people\" were provisionally detained as part of a police operation on suspicion of severe breaches of the peace and attacks on law enforcement.\n\"Here there were isolated attempts to escape the police cordon,\" police said, adding that underage individuals were among those suspected and that they were prioritizing processing those cases.\nWhat are the demonstrators upset about?\nThe activists are demonstrating in the aftermath of\na young woman from Leipzig, identified as Lina E., receiving a 5-year jail term earlier in the week for her part in organized attacks on neo-Nazis in Germany\n. She also received a last-minute reprieve of sorts, when the judge said she would only have to serve the remainder of her jail sentence should she also lose at appeal.\nThe scheduled date for the verdict had been known for months, and Lina's supporters evidently were not optimistic about her chances of acquittal, as they had been drumming up support for protests on the following weekend since last year.\nThey referred to it as \"day X\" (\nTag X\n), a term often used in German to describe a moment in the future that you believe will be somehow decisive or crucial.\nThey wanted to stage the march under a motto that roughly translates as \"United we stand — defend autonomous antifascists, in spite of all this.\"\nLina E. hails from Leipzig, one of the three German cities — along with Berlin and Hamburg — that law enforcement often cite as the hotspots for radical left-wing activism\nImage: Bernd März/IMAGO\nOvernight clashes\nPolice and firefighters already had their hands full overnight on Friday, clashing with protesters in some parts of the city. By morning, they were still extinguishing fires set by vandals and counting the costs of property damage.\nImpromptu demonstrations took place and then turned violent as local courts upheld a ban on the larger protest march planned for Saturday.\nPolice said that as many as 700 people had gathered in the south of the city and that many had thrown objects at law enforcement, some of them from balconies and rooftops.\n\"Barricades were erected and fires were lit,\" Leipzig police said in a statement. \"By the current count, the police has 23 lightly wounded officers and 17 damaged vehicles.\" They also noted comparatively major damage at one branch of the Sparkasse bank.\nPolice were out in force on Saturday, anticipating more unrest\nImage: Hendrik Schmidt/dpa/picture alliance\nWhy did they need permission?\nMajor protests in Germany require authorization from authorities. Local governments and courts can restrict them if they deem that the event could pose a risk to public safety, among other reasons.\n\nLeipzig city authorities and two courts in the city ruled that Saturday's demonstration should not go ahead, saying that advanced online appeals calling on people to protest either showed a tendency towards violent acts or sometimes even included explicit calls for violence.\n\"Leipzig's Higher Administrative Court is also of the conviction, that the city of Leipzig plausibly predicted that the protests could turn violent and therefore pose a threat to the broader public,\" Leipzig's Higher Administrative court said late on Friday when upholding the first court decision finding that the march should not be allowed.\nThe German Constitutional Court, meanwhile, said on Saturday that it would not hear a last-gasp emergency injunction on the issue, meaning the protesters had exhausted their legal avenues.\nPolice had also said that the estimated turnout from organizers of between 400 and 500 people did not seem realistic, given the far larger turnouts — including 3,500 people in 2021, when Lina E.'s trial opened — for past protests with much more muted and shorter periods trying to mobilize support online.\nAdvertisement\nProtests also coincided with Leipzig's bid for German Cup\nLeipzig police might have anticipated a busy Saturday even prior to Lina E.'s verdict and the aftermath.\nRB Leipzig played Eintracht Frankfurt in the German Cup (or\nDFB Pokal\n) football final in Berlin on Saturday evening. Although many of Leipzig's most involved supporters might have traveled to the capital for the game, interest on the streets in the city is also liable to be great.\nIt also coincided with a march by the environmental protest group calling itself Last Generation in the city, and some other demonstrations that law enforcement saw no reason to block.\nCity police said that after Friday's unrest, checks were still being carried out at some points in the city.\n\"Police have made it their target for today, as well, to secure all demonstrations that take place and to be able to deal with any possibly violent gathering or mobilization. Police helicopters will again be used today to monitor and direct operations. The previous night confirmed how necessary this was,\" police wrote.\nmsh/nm (AFP, dpa)\nAdvertisement\nSkip next section More on Law and Justice from Europe\nMore on Law and Justice from Europe\nBerlin Pride attack: Are German juvenile courts too lenient?\nThe terror attack on Berlin's Pride event has put the work of juvenile courts into the spotlight.\nLaw and Justice\n07/30/2026\nJuly 30, 2026\nGermany: How effective are exit programs for Islamists?\nFollowing the Islamist terror attack in Berlin, the efficacy of efforts to deradicalize agitators is being questioned.\nLaw and Justice", "excerpt_sha256": "9bee6c4a1b65f92913e95e92ddac999343d1c01564c5eec3c00580cbd1cec350", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0007-E005", "source_id": "SRC-19786562f216960f", "url": "https://www.tagesschau.de/inland/deutschlandtrend/deutschlandtrend-3410.html", "title": "ARD-DeutschlandTrend: Zufriedenheit mit Kanzler Scholz auf Rekordtief", "publisher": "tagesschau.de", "raw_sha256": "3773c1bc1230883b778ea29653984defcab79837377fb6016a9fe97481d4ad6b", "fact_summary": "After the Federal Constitutional Court declared the transfer of Corona funds to the Climate and Transformation Fund unconstitutional, the government was compelled to prepare a supplementary 2023 budget and postponed adoption of the regular 2024 budget; -1 im Vgl. zum August 2023) erwartet davon keine große Veränderung: Die Regierungsarbeit sei dann \"ähnlich gut\" bzw. \"ähnlich schlecht\". Fast jeder Vierte (24 Prozent; +5) traut einer unionsgeführten Bundesregierung eine bessere Lösung aktueller Probleme zu. 17 Prozent (-4) rechnen mit schlechteren Ergebnissen einer unionsgeführten Regierung. Unions-Anhänger sind mittlerweile mehrheitlich überzeugt, dass unter Führung der Union die Probleme angepackt werden würden (60 Prozent). Bei den Anhängern anderer Parteien herrscht diesbezüglich weiterhin Skepsis.\nWie umgehen mit der Haushaltslücke?\nSeitdem das Bundesverfassungsgericht Mitte November die Übertragung von Corona-Mitteln in den Klima- und Transformationsfonds für verfassungswidrig erklärte, ringen die Ampelpolitiker um eine Lösung der Haushaltskrise. Dieses Urteil zwingt die Bundesregierung zu einem Nachtragshaushalt für 2023, die Verabschiedung des regulären Haushalts für 2024 wurde verschoben.\nMit Blick auf das kommende Jahr ist fast die Hälfte der Wahlberechtigten (47 Prozent) der Ansicht, dass der Bund am ehesten mit Einsparungen bei Ausgaben, Vorhaben und Investitionen reagieren sollte. 35 Prozent würden am ehesten die Aussetzung der Schuldenbremse durch die Feststellung einer erneuten Notlage unterstützen. Nur 8 Prozent würden die Anhebung von Steuern und Abgaben befürworten. Auch gegenüber Kürzungen bei Militärhilfen für die Ukraine zeigt sich eine Mehrheit der Befragten (54 Prozent) offen.\nEinsparungen bei der Klimatransformation der Wirtschaft befürworten nur vier von zehn Befragten (41 Prozent) und nur 27 Prozent sehen Einsparpotential bei der geplanten Kindergrundsicherung. Nur einer von zehn Befragten (10 Prozent, -4 im Vgl. zu November 2021) traut der Staatengemeinschaft diese Aufgabe zu.\nUnd wie schnell soll es dabei in Deutschland gehen, damit das Land seine Klimaziele erreichen kann? Hier ergibt sich ein uneinheitliches Bild: Eine relative Mehrheit von 38 Prozent gibt an, dass ihnen die Veränderungen im Klimaschutz in Deutschland zu langsam gehen (+2 im Vgl. zu Juli 2023). Jeder Vierte (25 Prozent) ist der Ansicht, dass das Tempo der Veränderungen gerade richtig ist (+5). Für 28 Prozent geht das Veränderungstempo in Sachen Klimaschutz zu schnell (-5).\nMehrheit sieht beim Klimaschutz Industrie in der Pflicht\nMit Blick auf die persönlichen Einstellungen der Befragten zum Klimaschutz geben sieben von zehn Befragten (70 Prozent) an, dass der Klimawandel ohne Einschränkungen in unserem Lebensstil nicht gestoppt werden kann (-15 im Vgl. zu Mai 2019). 62 Prozent stimmen der Aussage zu, dass Deutschland schon viel gegen den Klimawandel tut und deshalb erst einmal andere Länder mehr tun sollten.\n61 Prozent sind der Ansicht, dass beim Klimaschutz eher die Industrie als einzelne Konsumenten in der Pflicht sind (-7), 60 Prozent stimmen der Aussage zu, dass in der Debatte um die Klimapolitik zu viel Angst geschürt wird (+7).\nEine Minderheit von 36 Prozent ist der Ansicht, dass die ökologischen Kosten für Produkte, Güter und Dienstleistungen stärker in den Preis einfließen sollten, etwa in Form einer CO2-Steuer (-6 im Vgl. zu November 2022). Eine Mehrheit von 58 Prozent spricht sich dagegen aus. Nur noch drei von zehn Befragten (30 Prozent) sind der Ansicht, dass wir auf Wirtschaftswachstum verzichten sollten, um den Klimawandel zu stoppen (-16 im Vgl. zu November 2022), fast zwei Drittel (64 Prozent) stimmen dieser Aussage nicht zu.\nUntersuchungsanlage\nGrundgesamtheit:\nWahlberechtigte in Deutschland\nErhebungsmethode:\nZufallsbasierte Telefon- und Online-Befragung (davon 60 Prozent Festnetz, 40 Prozent Mobilfunk)\nErhebungszeitraum:\n04. - 06. Das Gesetz enthält keine Bestimmungen, um Unternehmen vor Gericht haftbar zu machen, und ist nur begrenzt auf die beteiligten Unternehmen in der Wertschöpfungskette anwendbar.\nRechte von Frauen und Mädchen\nNach den im Juli\nveröffentlichten\nRegierungsstatistiken registrierte die Polizei im Jahr 2022 240.547 Fälle von häuslicher Gewalt, was einem Anstieg von 8,5 Prozent gegenüber 2021 entspricht. Das deutsche Innenministerium\nstellte sein Ausbildungsprogramm\nfür saudische Grenztruppen ein, nachdem Berichte über die Ermordung hunderter äthiopischer Migrant*innen an der Grenze zum Jemen durch saudische Kräfte bekannt wurden.\nIm Menschenrechtsrat der Vereinten Nationen hat Deutschland weiterhin eine wichtige Führungsrolle in einer Reihe von Schlüsselfragen übernommen, unter anderem bei einer\nResolution\n\nzur Sicherstellung einer kontinuierlichen Prüfung der russischen Menschenrechtsbilanz und einer weiteren Resolution zur Einleitung einer Untersuchung der schweren Menschenrechtsverletzungen im\nSudan-Konflikt\n. Deutschland hat es jedoch versäumt, ernsthafte Anstrengungen zu unternehmen\n, um die Wiederaufnahme einer entscheidenden Untersuchung zu Äthiopien zu gewährleisten\n, und es hat sich einer Minderheit von Ländern angeschlossen, die gegen eine\nResolution\nzu Rassismus, rassistischer Diskriminierung, Fremdenfeindlichkeit und damit zusammenhängender Intoleranz gestimmt haben.\nDeutschland äußerte menschenrechtliche Bedenken hinsichtlich des im Juli angekündigten Migrationsabkommens zwischen der EU und Tunesien, ergriff jedoch keine konkreten Maßnahmen, um die Umsetzung des Abkommens von Verbesserungen im Bereich der Menschenrechte abhängig zu machen.\nIm Februar\nbrachten\nsieben UN-Sonderberichterstatter*innen\nihre große Besorgnis\ndarüber zum\nAusdruck\n, dass Deutschland seinen Verpflichtungen gemäß den internationalen Menschenrechtsnormen nicht nachkommt. Sie forderten einen menschenrechtsorientierten Wiedergutmachungsprozess, der eine sinnvolle Beteiligung und Vertretung der betroffenen Gemeinschaften einschließt, um die anhaltenden Auswirkungen der während der deutschen Kolonialherrschaft in Namibia begangenen Verbrechen anzugehen.\nDeutschland ist einer der Hauptaktionäre des Internationalen Währungsfonds (IWF). Aber es hat keine Schritte unternommen, um die IWF-Politik zu reformieren, die Menschenrechte bedroht, wie z.B. die Konditionierung seiner Unterstützung für Sparprogramme, die keine Vorabbewertung der menschenrechtlichen Auswirkungen beinhalten, die niedrige Sozialausgaben fördern oder die bedarfsorientierte statt universelle soziale Sicherheit fördern.\nLänder durchsuchen\nAuswählen\nDeutschland\nEuropäische Union\nAktuelle Informationen auf Englisch über Menschenrechte weltweit\nLeave blank\nLeave blank\nLeave blank\nRegistrieren", "excerpt_sha256": "140dd63a237dd67d1b281bd8c3242ab5e786311acf6a21b30ea70b145da66c25", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0009-E012", "source_id": "SRC-60a3eb49fd3d4236", "url": "https://www.bundestag.de/dokumente/textarchiv/2023/kw38-de-onlinezugangsgesetz-965016", "title": "Kritik am Stand der Verwaltungsdigitalisierung", "publisher": "Deutscher Bundestag", "raw_sha256": "adc350cca41a0fac234b5b8bcfd7b40d87c7aa5b8e62639b4942e6365aaf1cb9", "fact_summary": "At the October 2023 hearing, experts reported that only 105 of the 575 services targeted for online access by the end of 2022 were online at the previous year-end and agreed that the original Online Access Act had missed its target; they also found the 2023 amendment insufficient, citing absent standards, timelines, cost clarity, independent monitoring, security rules, and a common technical model.", "question_ids": ["5B.6", "5B.7", "8B.5", "8B.6", "8B.8"], "polarity": "adverse", "period_fit": "2023 implementation assessment and hearing", "ruler_attribution": "Independent experts assessing the federal government's digital-administration programme", "limitations": ["The implementation shortfall began under the 2017 law and is not solely attributable to Scholz."], "start_unit": 33, "end_unit": 36, "locator": "extracted HTML block 33 through extracted HTML block 36", "exact_excerpt": "Kritik am Stand der Verwaltungsdigi­talisierung\nZeit:\nMontag, 9. Oktober 2023, 14 Uhr\nOrt:\nBerlin, Paul-Löbe-Haus, Sitzungssaal E 600\nSachverständige aus Wirtschaft, Wissenschaft, Zivilgesellschaft und Kommunalverbänden haben am\nMontag, 9. Oktober 2023,\ndie Forderung bekräftigt, die Digitalisierung der öffentlichen Verwaltung mit Nachdruck voranzutreiben. Dazu sei der vorliegende Entwurf einer Novelle des\nOnlinezugangsgesetzes\n(OZG) (\n20/8093\n(Dokument, öffnet ein neues Fenster)\n) zwar ein „Schritt in die richtige Richtung“, aber nach wie vor unzureichend, hieß es in einer Anhörung im Ausschuss für Inneres und Heimat.\nMit dem 2017 verabschiedeten Onlinezugangsgesetz hatten sich Bund und Länder die Aufgabe gestellt, den Bürgern bis Ende 2022 ein Paket von 575 staatlichen Dienstleistungen auf digitalem Wege zugänglich zu machen. Zum vergangenen Jahreswechsel waren davon indes nur 105 Angebote online geschaltet, mittlerweile sind es einige mehr. Dass das Gesetz sein Ziel bisher verfehlt hat, war Konsens unter den Sachverständigen. Umstritten war, ob die Einführung eines gesetzlichen Rechtsanspruchs auf digitale Verwaltungsleistungen, wie ihn die Unionsfraktion in einem Antrag (\n20/4313\n(Dokument, öffnet ein neues Fenster)\n) fordert, ein geeignetes Mittel der Beschleunigung sei.\nStaatliches Handeln im digitalen Zeitalter\nDer ehemalige Bevollmächtigte des Saarlandes für Innovation und Strategie,\nAmmar Alkassar\n, würdigte in der Anhörung das OZG „trotz aller Defizite“ als bisher umfangreichste Initiative, um staatliches Handeln auf das digitale Zeitalter einzustellen. Die jetzt vorliegende Novelle sei „positiv“, bleibe aber „hinter den Erfordernissen einer raschen Verwaltungsdigitalisierung“ zurück, sagte Alkassem. Es fehlten nicht nur „einige grundlegende Bausteine“, sondern ein „gemeinsames Zielbild“ im Sinne eines „plattformbasierten Ökosystems der öffentlichen Hand“. Zudem sei „Überkomplexität“ abzubauen: „Eine konsequente Umsetzung der Vewaltungsdigitalisierung erfordert einen echten Einschnitt“, mahnte Alkassem.\n„Nach fünf Jahren OZG ist Deutschland nicht dort, wo es in der Verwaltungsdigitalisierung sein sollte“, klagte\nGrudrun Aschenbrenner\nvon\nVorstand der Anstalt für kommunale Datenverarbeitung in Bayern\n\n. Sie wünsche sich „disruptive Änderungen“ und mehr Mut. In anderen Ländern gelte längst das Prinzip, Verwaltungsvorgänge nur noch digital abzuwickeln: „Was hält uns davon ab?“\nStandards, Zielsetzung und Kosten\nAls Vertreterin des\nDeutschen Städtetages\nwies\nUda Bastians\nauf die zunehmende Personalnot der öffentlichen Verwaltung bei wachsenden Anforderungen hin, die die Umstellung auf digitale Verfahren zu einem zwingenden Gebot werden ließen. Bis 2030 werde ein Drittel der derzeit Beschäftigten in den Ruhestand gehen. Zugleich drehe sich das Karussell der Gesetzesnovellen immer schneller. So sei allein das Aufenthaltsrecht seit 2015 nicht weniger als 59 Mal geändert worden: „Die Digitalisierung ist der Schlüssel, um das Land am Laufen zu halten.“ Gemessen daran bleibe der vorliegende Entwurf hinter den Erwartungen zurück. Es fehlten klare Festlegungen auf Standards, Zielsetzung und Kosten.\nFür den\nDeutschen Landkreistag\nwies\nAriane Berger\ndie Forderung nach einem Rechtsanspruch auf digitale Verwaltungsleistungen, der nach Vorstellung der Union 2025 in Kraft treten soll, als untauglich zurück. Die kommunale IT müsse für die Erfordernisse einer weitgehenden Digitalisierung „aufnahmefähig“ gemacht werden. Es bedürfe eines „Standardisierungprogramms“ für die Verwaltung.\nRechtsanspruch auf digitale Verwaltungsleistungen\n„Grundsätzlich begrüßenswert“, aber nicht ausreichend, um das „\nE-Government\n-Entwicklungsland“ in einen „digitalen Staat“ zu verwandeln, nannte\nJonas Botta\nvom\nDeutschen Forschungsinstitut für öffentliche Verwaltung in Speyer\nden vorliegenden Gesetzentwurf und empfahl, ihn „nachzuschärfen“. Die Novelle müsse, wie das bisherige OZG, erneut eine Frist für die Umsetzung enthalten. Es bedürfe klarer Kriterien, um den Realisierungsfortschritt durch eine unabhängige Stelle regelmäßig evaluieren zu lassen. Nicht zuletzt erklärte Botta einen Rechtsanspruch auf digitale Verwaltungsleistungen für sinnvoll.\nAuch\nMarc Danneberg\nvon\nBranchenverband Bitcom\nsah hier eine Möglichkeit, um den „Umsetzungsdruck“ zu erhöhen. Das Gesetz müsse „Planungssicherheit“ gewährleisten sowie Nutzern einen „niedrigschwelligen“ und flexiblen Zugang zu digitalen Verwaltungsleistungen ermöglichen. Es müsse auch unterschiedliche Wege eröffnen, um Zahlungen an Behörden zu leisten.\n\n„International einmaliges Gesetz“\nAnnette Guckelberger, Professorin für Öffentliches Recht an der Universität des Saarlandes,\nerklärte es für „vertretbar“, dass der Entwurf im Unterschied zum bisherigen OZG keine „pauschale Umsetzungsfrist“ enthält. Es müsse aber einen Zeitplan geben und jährliche Fortschrittsberichte, die an den Bundestag zu erstatten seien. Einen Rechtsanspruch halte sie nicht für sinnvoll, sagte Guckelberger. Digitalisierung sei ein komplexer Prozess, das OZG darin nur einer von mehreren Schritten. Auch die Länder seien gefragt, Regelungen zu beschließen.\nDas OZG sei gescheitert, stellte\nProfessor Gerhard Hammerschmid\nvon der\nBerliner Hertie-\nSchool\nfest. Es sei in seiner Zeit zwar ein „international einmaliges Gesetz“ gewesen, aber mit einem so hohen Anspruch, dass es „zum Scheitern verurteilt“ gewesen sei. Hammerschmid sprach sich gegen einen Rechtsanspruch aus, der „administrativ nicht umsetzbar“ sei, dafür umso nachdrücklicher für ein „verbindliches und transparentes“ Monitoring durch unabhängige Experten, zumal, da es künftig keine verpflichtende Umsetzungsfrist mehr geben solle.\nKompetenzen zwischen Bund und Ländern\n„Wir müssen uns gemeinsam auf die zentralen Dinge konzentrieren und das umsetzen, was schnell Nutzen bringt“, mahnte\nAlexander Handschuh\nvom\nDeutschen Städte- und Gemeindebund\n. Er wandte sich gegen die Absicht des Gesetzgebers, mit dem vorliegende Entwurf die Kommunen direkt statt allein der Länder für die Digitalisierung in die Pflicht zu nehmen. Dies schaffe „keinen Mehrwert“, ebenso wenig wie die Forderung nach einem Rechtsanspruch.\nInga Karrer\nvon der\nKoordinationsstelle\nE-Governmen\nt bei der Deutschen Industrie- und Handelsammer\ngab zu bedenken, dass eine erfolgreiche Digitalisierungsstrategie möglicherweise bedeute, die Kompetenzen zwischen Bund und Ländern im Grundgesetz neu zu verteilen. Sie erfordere ein Maß an Zentralisierung, das nach aktueller Verfassungslage nicht zulässig sei.\nSicherheit der Nutzerkonten und der Postfächer\nAls „Teil der digitalen Zivilgesellschaft“, deren Leben mit der Gesetzesnovelle „schneller und einfacher“ werden solle, stellte sich\nBianca Kastl\nvom\n\nBerliner Innovationsverbund Öffentliche Gesundheit\nvor. Sie bemängelte fehlende Klarheit und Transparenz im vorliegenden Entwurf. Dieser können „allenfalls ein Auftaktprozess einer zukunftsfähigen Verwaltung sein, die bei den Menschen ankommt, mehr leider nicht“.\n„Klare gesetzliche Vorgaben für die Sicherheit der Nutzerkonten und der Postfächer“ mahnte der Bundesdatenschutzbeauftragte Ulrich Kelber an. Auch er stellte fest, Deutschland sei „gefährlich unterdigitalisiert“. Jörg Kremer als Vertreter der Föderalen IT-Kooperation, einer in Frankfurt ansässigen Anstalt öffentlichen Rechts, die Bund und Länder bei der Verwaltungsdigitalisierung unterstützen soll, nannte das bestehende OZG „suboptimal“, aber doch zugleich eine „Initialzündung“ für digitalen Wandel. Erforderlich sei, die oftmals zu trägen Entscheidungs- und Abstimmungsprozesse in der öffentlichen Verwaltung der digitalen Welt anzupassen. Malte Spitz von Nationalen Normenkontrollrat warnte, dass auch die OZG-Novelle scheitern könnte, wenn sie nicht nachgebessert werde.\nGesetzentwurf der Bundesregierung\nMit dem Gesetzentwurf sollen die neu geschaffenen Strukturen der Bund-Länder-Zusammenarbeit verstetigt und eine einfache, moderne und digitale Verfahrensabwicklung im übergreifenden Portalverbund ermöglicht werden. Dafür will der Bund unter anderem zentrale Basisdienste bereitstellen und so landeseigene Entwicklungen für das Bürgerkonto und das Postfach ersetzen. Außerdem soll ein schriftformersetzendes qualifiziertes elektronisches Siegel und eine Regelung zu\nDigital-Only\nfür Unternehmensleistungen eingeführt werden.\nDer Nutzen des Gesetzentwurfs besteht aus Sicht der Bundesregierung darin, „dass er den Prozess der Entwicklung nutzerfreundlicher digitaler\nServices\nweiter fördert“. Dies vereinfache das Leben der Menschen und die Tätigkeit von Unternehmen und Selbständigen, soweit diese staatliche Leistungen in Anspruch nähmen.\nAntrag der Union\nDie Unionsfraktion fordert eine gesetzliche Nachfolgeregelung, die die Fortsetzung des auslaufenden Onlinezugangsgesetzes (OZG) sichert. In ihrem Antrag heißt es, es brauche einen rechtlichen, zeitlichen und finanzieller Rahmen für ein OZG 2.0, um den Ländern und Kommunen Planungssicherheit zu geben. Der IT-Planungsrat müsse vor Beginn des formalen Gesetzgebungsverfahren in ausreichender Form beteiligt werden, schreibt die Fraktion weiter. Die Bundesregierung solle zudem einen Rechtsanspruch der Bürgerinnen und Bürger ab dem 1. Januar 2025 auf die im OZG definierten Verwaltungsleistungen des Bundes vorlegen.\nIm Hinblick auf den Umsetzungsrückstand zum Fristablauf des OZG soll die Bundesregierung sich dafür einsetzen, dass „einzelne verfügbare Online-Leistungen auch flächendeckend für alle Nutzerinnen und Nutzer beschleunigt umgesetzt werden“, um eine Zersplitterung des Bereitstellungangebotes zu verhindern, fordert die Fraktion. Über eine gezielte Kommunikationsstrategie in Zusammenarbeit mit allen OZG-Dienstleistern müsse zudem der Bekanntheitsgrad und das Nutzungsverhalten von digitalen Verwaltungsdienstleistungen bei den Bürgerinnen und Bürgern unterstützt werden. (wid/joh/lbr/ste/09.10.2023)\nDokumente\n20/4313 - Antrag: Mehr Tempo bei digitaler Verwaltung - Onlinezugangsgesetz fortführen, Nutzungslücken schließen, Rechtsanspruch einführen\nPDF\n| 161 KB — Status: 08.11.2022\n(Dokument, Link öffnet ein neues Fenster)\n20/8093 - Gesetzentwurf: Entwurf eines Gesetzes zur Änderung des Onlinezugangsgesetzes sowie weiterer Vorschriften zur Digitalisierung der Verwaltung (OZG-Änderungsgesetz - OZGÄndG)\nPDF\n| 1 MB — Status: 23.08.2023\n(Dokument, Link öffnet ein neues Fenster)\nTagesordnung\n51. A speaker of the\nForeigner’s Office declared that there were no evidence supporting a systemic misuse of the admission\nprograms.782 Only in one case a person who applied for admission has been identified as a possible\n‘threat’ (‘Gefährder’). Nevertheless, the Foreigners Office along with the Minister of Interior decided to\nintroduce additional screening mechanisms and to halt the admission program until the screening\nmechanism is in place.783 The screening procedure involves an automatic data reconciliation with\ndatabases from the national security agencies and interviews with the German domestic intelligence\nservice (Bundesamt für Verfassungsschutz), Federal Criminal Police Office (Bundeskriminalamt), the\nFederal Police or BAMF officers acting on behalf of these national security services.784 The admission\nprograms were restarted from 26 June 2023. In the first months of the newly introduced screening\nprocedure (26 June – 21 July 2023) 99 screening interviews took place, no security concerns have been\nidentified among those.785 The Federal government further states, that the capacities for the screening\nmechanism have been expanded since its start. Accordingly, as of August 2023 several hundreds of\nscreenings can be conducted per month.786\n\nThe Left party and NGOs such as PRO ASYL welcomed the launch of the programme but criticised that\n1,000 admissions per month was too low given the ‘real pressure of persecution’ for ‘people who have\nfought for democracy and human rights’. PRO ASYL further criticised that the relatively abstract selection\ncriteria could lead to an ‘ethically highly ambivalent protection lottery’787 especially in connection with the\nfact that only authorised agencies could put forward people and that the application is through an\nalgorithm-based IT application with little room to put forward specific individual circumstances.788 The\nNGO Kabul Luftbrücke reported problems with the IT application in October and November, leading to\ndelays in sending the online forms to authorities. A further point of critique is that the programme does not\nextend to persons who have managed to flee Afghanistan.789 Several NGOs also voiced concerns over\nthe practical implementation, demanding a better staffing of the counselling and coordination centres for\nthe programme and questioning the ‘organization and content’ of the procedure,790 especially given that\nit is required to have a passport in order to leave the country while obtaining one is made extremely\ndifficult by the Taliban government. One year after the official launch, in October 2023, several NGOs\nreflected on the development of the program. They demanded that the originally envisaged number of\n1,000 admissions per months should be fulfilled, that the procedure should be more transparent and that\nin additional admission schemes are necessary to meet the needs for protection.791\n\nIn addition to the Federal Government, several Federal States (Thuringia, Berlin, Hessen and Bremen)\nhave implemented admission programmes based on family ties to Afghans living in the respective Federal\nStates (for more information see Family Reunification). however, access to such programmes is\ndifficult in practice, especially for persons who are still in Afghanistan.793\n\nAsylum applications of Afghan nationals in Germany\n\nIn 2023, the protection rate for Afghan nationals stayed at a high of 98.7% compared to 99.3 % in 2022.794\nPrior to the takeover of Taliban it more than doubled. It was 42.9% in 2021 and 36.6% in 2020.795 Most\nAfghan nationals were given either humanitarian protection in the form of a national removal ban (50.3%)\nor 43.8% of applicants were given refugee status. As of mid-August 2021, the BAMF de-prioritised\ndecisions on asylum applications from Afghanistan due to the uncertain situation in the country except for\ncases in which international protection can be granted according to the guidelines in place or where the\nsituation in Afghanistan was irrelevant for the decision. The government further declared that decisions\ncontinued to be taken on an individual, case-by-case basis.796 As a result, the number of pending\napplications by Afghan nationals rose considerably compared to 2020, to 27,846 at the end of 2021 (2020:\n6,101).The BAMF resumed decisions concerning Afghan nationals in December 2021,797 prioritising\ncases which involve several persons (as opposed to individual applications) and vulnerable applicants.798\nAt the end of 2023, the number of pending cases was still high with 39,000 undecided cases (among\nwhich 37,566 first-time and 1,434 subsequent applications). 2023 also saw a relatively high number of\ndecisions on subsequent applications from Afghan nationals (4,622), which in most cases led to the\ngranting of some form of protection (see Subsequent applications).\n\nThe already high success rate of appeals before Administrative Courts against negative decisions in the\nasylum procedure increased considerably in 2023. From the start of 2023 until the end of August 2023,\n618 Afghan nationals were granted a form of protection by courts, compared to 194 rejections of appeals.\nIn total, 21.6% of appeals were successful in the first half of 2023 (the rate was 40.6% in 2022 and 45.2%\nin 2021). If only decisions on the merits are counted, 76.1% of appeals resulted in the granting of\nprotection (2022: 94.8%, 2021: 77.8%).799 Most of the cases were not decided on the merits but resolved\nin other ways such as completion (71.6%). In the first half of 2023, the appeal statistics show large\ndifferences between courts. Whereas the administrative court Greifswald (Mecklenburg – Western\nPomerania) has a positive decision rate of 6.5%, the administrative court of Augsburg (Bavaria) has 0%\npositive decisions.800 20,496 appeals of Afghan nationals were pending at the court as of 31 May 2023. A\nconsiderable increase compared to the number of appeals in 2022 (7,546).801\n\n\n\n\n792\n      Pro Asyl, Afghanistan – Landesaufnahmeprogramme, 30 October 2023, available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/47Y7c5H.\n793\n      For an overview of such existing programmes see Hammed Hakimi, Higher Education in Europe: A Pathway\n      to Protection for Afghans?, ECRE Working Paper 17, November 2022, available at: https://bit.ly/42igPuu.\n794\n      BAMF, Antrags-, Entscheidungs- und Bestandsstatistik, 8 January 2024, available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/3UjFWf0.\n795\n      For more information about decision making in previous years, see AIDA, Country Report Germany - Update\n      on the year 2021, April 2022, available at: https://bit.ly/3XnN7RS, 91-92.\n796\n      Federal Government, Response to parliamentary question by The Left, 19/32678, 14 October 2021, available\n      in German at: https://bit.ly/3RQINtZ, 18-19.\n797\n      See PRO ASYL, ‘Steigende Asylzahlen? The decree also mentions that immigration authorities should issue\nresidence permits directly without a preceding visa procedure for persons who are already in Germany in\ncases where a return to Russia would put applicants in danger.824 For persons who do not fulfil the criteria\nfor a residence title in Germany or for being granted international protection, PRO ASYL assumes that\nthey should be issued a tolerated stay (Duldung) on the basis that removals to Russia are currently\nimpossible.825 Despite the decree in 2022 to grant special rights to Russian cultural and media workers,\nRussian journalists who fled to Germany report that they often only received tolerated stay (Duldung),\nwhich forces them to stay in Germany without possibilities to secure their livelihood and to continue their\nwork as journalists.826\n\nIn the first half of 2023 no person was removed to Russia and no person with Russian nationality was\nremoved involuntarily from Germany.827\n\n      5. Palestinian territories\n\nThe attack by the Hamas on Israel on the 7 October 2023 and the following escalating conflict has led to\npolitical discussions and rifts in the public perception. Following the attack, chancellor Scholz declared\nthe security of Israel as a reason of state for Germany. He claimed that Germany’s place is on the side of\nIsrael and that Germany stands in full solidarity and supports Israel.828 The German government continues\nto position itself in favour for Israel, e.g., by its abstention to the UN resolution and its rejection of an EU\nresolution on ceasefire. Only very wary and situational criticism to the Israelian government and its\nreaction to the attack by the German government have been voiced by the German government. At a\npress conference of the Foreigners Office on 9 October 2023, the speaker of the Foreigners Office\nmentioned that in the past the German government has voiced criticism to the Israelian handling of the\nmiddle-east conflict and generally envisages a two-state solution but that at the current moment the focus\nshould be the support of Israel’s defence.829 Since then, the Minister of Foreign Affairs increasingly raises\ncriticism to specific actions of the Israelian government, e.g., calling for humanitarian corridors and\nsupport, demanding the protection of civil society and the adherence to International Humanitarian Law.830\nNevertheless, the German government has announced to support Israel in the case pending before the\nInternational Criminal Court.831 The public perception is rifted. At the same time, it has been reported that pro-Palestinian demonstrations\nhave been prohibited per se without any distinction to the cause they were protesting for, which amounted\nto a violation of the equal freedom of assembly. Additionally, the police have been criticised for its brutal\nreactions against pro-Palestinian demonstrations being in parts racially motivated.832 Some associations\nlike Jews and Palestinians for Peace and Combatants for Peace try to lead the public debate back to the\nfacts and a constructive exchange in providing information and workshops for schools and other\nassociations.833\n\nWhat impact the situation in the Palestinian territories and the political climate here in Germany have on\nPalestinian refugees in Germany is currently difficult to evaluate. The number of 743 asylum applicants\nin 2023 from Palestinian territories has significantly increased compared to 35 in 2022.834 This could be a\nresult of the escalating conflict. Another contributing factor to the increase of asylum applicants might\nhave been the court rulings of the Administrative Court in Oldenburg and of the Court of Justice of the\nEU. The Administrative Court in Oldenburg decided already in June 2023, prior to the escalation, that the\ncurrent situation in the West Bank amounts to a danger to the health and life of those living there and that\ntherefore persons present in Germany are eligible for toleration (‘Duldung’) under national law.835 The\nEuropean Court of Human Rights affirmed that UNRWA does no longer guarantee protection for\nPalestinians, making them eligible for national protection.836 Looking at the political debate, it seems that\nthere is a harsh climate not only with regard to the middle-east conflict but also vis-à-vis Palestinian\nrefugees. The Christian Democrats (CDU) affirmed that while humanitarian aid will be provided, migration\nflows to Germany should be prevented. The Social Democrats stated that the right to claim asylum applies\nto everyone equally and that possible security threats are checked for Palestinians as for every other\nasylum applicant.837\n\n\n\n\n832\n      Clara Neumann, Das Spannungsverhältnis zwischen Staatsräson und Grundrechten, 8 December 2023,\n      available in German at: https://bit.ly/3St683X.\n833\n      Tagesschau.de, Die Hoffnung auf Frieden, 16 December 2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3w1d0ye;\n      WDR.de, Jüdin und Palästinenserin: “Ohne Dialog geht es nicht”, 19 November 2023, available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/3unv70F.\n834\n      BAMF, Asylgeschäftsstatistik (statistics on applications, decisions and pending procedures), 1-12/2023,\n      available at: htps://bit.ly/3UjFWf0 1-12/2022 available at: https://bit.ly/3Z7UIUM.\n835\n      Administrative Court Oldenburg, Decision 3 A 3611/21, 7 June 2023, available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/3waRhDM.\n836\n      CJEU, Decision C-294/22, 5 October 2023, available at: https://bit.ly/49noIBC.\n837\n      Rheinische Post.de, Union will keine palästinensischen Flüchtlinge, 18 October 2023, available in Germany\n      at: https://bit.ly/3up1I6n.\n\n\n                                                     129", "excerpt_sha256": "a3f1ae3ed27b6025d732038e85d8ff095c238f162a8ab7f03fe8d321e96684a5", "verification_status": "accepted", "verification_notes": []}, {"evidence_id": "BATCH-0011-R04-E006", "source_id": "SRC-a007cee33f0d9205", "url": "https://asylumineurope.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/AIDA-DE_2023-Update.pdf", "title": "Country Report: Germany; 2023 Update", "publisher": "ECRE / AIDA", "raw_sha256": "35a16fff9b7b255d989c2a5cc6e56b8de7d67ce65e07a782935d6cac97244469", "fact_summary": "In 2023 reception capacity was severely strained: Berlin and Hamburg were around 97% occupied, Berlin required 8,000 additional places, and overcrowded facilities produced reports of cold tents, illness, inadequate sanitation, lack of privacy, registration backlogs, insufficient health care, and violent conflicts; the federal government and states agreed on additional federal aid in November 2023.", "question_ids": ["3B.6", "3B.7", "3B.9", "5B.6", "5B.9", "6B.2", "6B.3", "6B.6", "8B.5", "8B.6", "8B.8", "8B.9"], "polarity": "mixed", "period_fit": "The capacity shortages, reported conditions, and November 2023 federal-state agreement concern 2023.", "ruler_attribution": "The report attributes the funding agreement to the federal government and federal states, while accommodation operations are primarily local and state responsibilities.", "limitations": ["The cited harms are drawn from multiple local reports and are not presented as a nationally standardized inspection result."], "start_unit": 151, "end_unit": 153, "locator": "page 151 through page 153", "exact_excerpt": "regulations provide for 6 or 7m² per person.971 A typical room in an initial reception centre has between 2\nand 4 beds, there are chairs and a table and each resident has a locker for themselves. Size of rooms\nmay vary, but rooms with a single bed are highly exceptional.\n\nMost initial reception centres have a policy to accommodate single women and families in separate\nbuildings or separate wings of their buildings. The AnKER centre in Manching/Ingolstadt for example\nprovides separate rooms for vulnerable persons.\n\nBath and toilet facilities usually consist of shower rooms and toilets which people have to share. As\nmentioned before, there are no regulations that oblige the Federal states to fulfil any standards. However,\nthe Federal Ministry for Family Affairs, Senior Citizens, Women and Youth and UNICEF have worked out\na policy paper stating minimum standards concerning the protection of refugees in reception centres.972\nThese guidelines state that sanitary facilities should be separate between genders and that those facilities\nshould be built in a way that guarantees privacy. Other guidelines recommend that one shower should be\navailable for 10 to 12 persons, but in some reception centres the ratio is worse than that, particularly in\nsituations of overcrowding. Cleaning of shared space (halls, corridors) as well as of sanitary facilities is\ncarried out by external companies in the initial reception centres.\n\nFood is supplied in the initial reception centres and is usually served in canteens on the premises of the\ncentres. In general, two or more menus are on offer for lunch and the management of the catering facilities\ntries to ensure that specific food is provided with regard to religious sentiments. Some, but not all initial\nreception centres also have shared kitchen space which enables asylum seekers to cook their own food;\nin AnkER centres, for instance, cooking is not allowed. Refrigerators for the use of asylum seekers are\navailable in some initial reception centres, but this seems to be the exception. In some centres, the\nmanagement does not allow hot water boilers for asylum seekers as this would be forbidden by fire\nregulations.973\n\nThe living conditions in many initial reception centres have been criticised by asylum seekers, volunteers\nand NGOs – especially in light of the extended obligatory stay in these facilities. In 2022 the conditions\ndeteriorated even more due to the massive overcrowding as consequence from the war in Ukraine and\nthe situation in Afghanistan. But still in 2023, small cities like Pulheim in North Rhine-Westphalia,974 as\nwell as middle size municipalities like Augsburg in Bavaria975 and Aachen in North Rhine-Westphalia976\nbut also big municipalities like Berlin977 and Hamburg978 face difficulties in accommodating new\nprotection seekers. In Berlin and Hamburg, around 97% percent of the reception capacities were\noccupied around October and November 2023.979\n\n\n\n\n971\n      European Migration Network, The Organisation of Reception Facilities for Asylum Seekers in Germany, 2013,\n      available in German at: https://bit.ly/48ojgO9, 26.\n972\n      Federal Ministry for Family Affairs, Senior Citizens, Women and Youth and UNICEF, Mindeststandards\n      zum Schutz von geflüchteten Menschen in Flüchtlingsunterkünften, available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/48WAaV2.\n973\n      PROASYL and Refugee Council Berlin, Das Asylbewerberleistungsgesetz – Einschränkungen des\n      Grundrechts auf ein menschenwürdiges Existenzminimum für Geflüchtete, November 2022, available in\n      German at: https://bit.ly/3XrdSox, 100.\n974\n       WDR.de, Geflüchtete klagen über menschenunwürdige Zustände in Pulheimer Flüchtlingsunterkünften,\n      available at: https://bit.ly/3SKjme1.\n975\n      Augsburger Allgemeine, Augsburg gerät bei der Unterbringung von Geflüchteten wieder stärker unter Druck,\n      19 August 2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/42MZ1Iq.\n976\n      InfoMigrants, ‘Refugees: German cities are reaching their limits’, 26 September 2022, available at:\n      https://bit.ly/3wpxjCp.\n977\n      Silke Mehring, Leichtbauhallen in Tegel geplant Unterbringung von Ukraine-Flüchtlingen in Berlin wird immer\n      komplizierter, rbb24, 02 December.2022, available in German at: http://bit.ly/3wdFPUT.\n978\n      Ibid.\n979\n      Rbb24.de, Berlin droht Verlust von tausenden Unterkunftsplätzen für Geflüchtete, 28 Novermber 2023,\n      available in German at: https://bit.ly/48nVWzN; NDR, Flüchtlingszahlen: Senat sieht Hamburg am Limit, 6\n      October 2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3SHfJ7w.\n\n\n                                                     151\n\nAccording to the administration of Berlin, 10,000 additional places were required in 2022.980 At the end\nof September 2023, the senate administration of Berlin spoke again of 8,000 more required places until\nthe end of 2023.981 Those necessary places are to be created in gymnasiums, hotels, exhibitions halls\nand through further usage of the old airport Tegel. In October 2023, the initial reception centre in Suhl,\nThuringia, was completely full and had imposed a freeze on admissions.982 However, the municipalities\ncannot offer any other accommodation facilities as all centres and apartments are full. The only possibility\nleft, according to the Thuringian Association of Towns and Municipalities, is the usage of sport halls.983\nMunicipalities in North Rine-Westphalia have started to rent hotels to provide accommodation for asylum\nseekers.984 Although this practice is very costly, it is preferred over blocking gymnasiums of local sport\nassociations. The authorities on the local, state and federal level blame each other for the shortcomings.\nWhile the local authorities are by law responsible for the accommodation of protection seekers,985 they\nclaim that the do not have enough financial and housing resources to fulfil the current need. They therefore\nask the Federal States to vacate more housing properties.986 The Federal States in turn urge the Federal\ngovernment to strengthen their efforts and to take up a coordinating role.987 After months of conflict, the\nFederal government and the Federal States have agreed in November 2023 – next to some deterrent\nmeasures that should decrease migration – upon more financial aid by the Federal Government.988\n\nAs consequence of the overcrowding, the authorities seem to be overburdened and deteriorating\nconditions have been reported. Under the law the state may derogate from the obligation to stay in initial\nreception centres in cases of overcrowding.989 Nevertheless, so far only Berlin has used this derogation\nclause and allows asylum seekers who have been allocated to Berlin under the “Köngisteiner Schlüssel”\nto live in private accommodations since the end of January 2023.990 In emergency shelters e.g. in Berlin,\nit has been reported that the tents at the former Berlin-Tegel airport do not protect from the cold causing\nnumerous illnesses and facilitating the spread of Covid-19 (on conditions in reception facilities during the\nCovid-19 pandemic see the 2021 Update).991 One partitioned area on such a tent is in general shared by\neight people. As a result, each asylum seeker has, according to calculations by the refugee council Berlin,\nonly about 2,6 m2 available for themselves.992 Additionally, since the airport is surrounded by barbed wire\nno systematic access for NGOs and volunteers is granted. At the same time protection seekers need to\ntake a shuttle bus to enter and exit the emergency shelter, thereby making it difficult for protection seekers\nto access legal aid and social assistance. In the emergency tents in Bremen protection seekers report of\nnon-functional and unclean sanitary facilities, coldness due to non-functional heating systems and a tense\natmosphere.993 Only one month later the municipality of Bremen decided to evacuate the tents due to the\n\n980\n      Thomas Frey, Ukraine-Ankunftszentrum wird um 3200 Plätze erweitert, Berliner Woche 06 January 2023,\n      available in German at: http://bit.ly/3CY9Jjy.\n981\n      Senatskanzlei Berlin, Mehr Plätze für die Unterbringung von Geflüchteten, press release from 26 September\n      2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3wnKSpd.\n982\n      Mdr.de, Erstaufnahmeeinrichtungen für Flüchtlinge – Was tun, wenn sie voll sind?, 6 October 2023, available\n      in German at: https://bit.ly/3I9iV74.\n983\n      Ibid.\n984\n      Wdr.de, Flüchtlinge in Kommunen: Letzter Ausweg Hotel, 26 January 2023, available in German at:\n      https://bit.ly/3UPlLFY.\n985\n      §44 AsylG in conjunction with the different Federal state’s Reception laws: e.g. §1 Landesaufnahmegesetz\n      Hesse; Inhabitants were partially relocated to emergency shelters on\nexhibition grounds. This solution was only temporary and local authorities have organised lightweight\nbuilding constructions which should serve until mid-2024.994 Even if asylum seekers do not live in tents\nbut in houses, the living conditions are in many cases catastrophic. In a reception centre in Pulheim,\nNorth Rhine-Westphalia, for example, the building smells heavily, the sanitary facilities are mouldy and\nrats run around the complex.995\n\nIn arrival centres, the overcrowding mostly leads to backlogs in the registration procedure and conflicts\namong the protection seekers stemming from the lack of privacy. Asylum seekers at the arrival centre in\nHamburg-Rahlstedt, for example, have reported inter alia a backlog of registration,996 lack of privacy,\nunclean sanitary facilities and disturbances at night. The sleeping areas are placed in former warehouses\nand divided by thin partitions into several compartments, which do not allow for privacy. Besides reading\nlamps attached to each bed, there is one common light for the whole warehouse, which is switched on\nfrom 8:00am to 22:00pm.997 A backlog of registration, lack of access to health care and social assistance\nhas been reported also from the arrival centre in Berlin.998 In the arrival centre in Thuringia, many violent\nconflicts have been reported stemming from the lack of staff members, stressed social workers and non-\ntrained security personnel.999 The backlog of registrations all over Germany has supposedly been caught\nup until the end of June 2023, according to the Federal Government.1000\n\nMore generally, studies published in 2020 have come to the conclusion that the accommodation in initial\nreception centres infringes upon children’s’ rights and constitutes a danger to their mental health. The\nspatial confinement, the experience of violence and removals, as well as the permanent uncertainty cause\npsychological stress and have a negative impact on children.1001 Health care and psychosocial support\nprovided for young refugees in the mass accommodations were described as worryingly inadequate for\nmost of the facilities.1002 The study of PROASYL and the Refugee Council Berlin support these findings.\nAccording to the study of 2022, especially the access to health care, access to adequate hygienic and\nother products such as strollers for toddlers are scarce.1003\n\nThe NGO ‘Ärzte der Welt’ (Doctors of the World) announced in September 2019 that an advice service\nrun by the organisation in the AnkER-centre of Manching/Ingolstadt was to be terminated. The NGO\ndescribed living conditions in the facility as ‘morbid’ and claimed that adequate treatment, in particular\ntreatment of persons with psychological disorders, was impossible under the circumstances. Insufficient\nprotection against assaults, lack of privacy and nocturnal disturbances were impeding the mental\nstabilisation of asylum-seekers at the facility and the NGO was no longer capable to bear responsibility\nfor the mental health of its patients. Moreover, the organisation claims that there was no system for the\nidentification of vulnerable persons in place at the facility.1004\n\n994\n       Weserreport, Geflüchtete ziehen um, 10 January 2023, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3wrtM9V.\n995\n       WDR.de, Geflüchtete klagen über menschenunwürdige Zustände in Pulheimer Flüchtlingsunterkünften,\n       available at: https://bit.ly/3SKjme1.\n996\n       Redaktionsnetzwerk Deutschland (RND), Streit in Hamburg: CDU wirft Senat unhaltbare Zustände in zentraler\n       Ankunftsstelle vor, 8 March 2022, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3XXoR9e.\n997\n       Fluchtpunkt, Mitten in Hamburg, und doch am Rand: Unzumutbare Bedingungen im Ankunftszentrum\n       Rahlstedt, November 14th 2019, available in German at: https://bit.ly/3iJgGtb.\n998\n       Taz.de, Ankunftszentrum überlaufen, 5 January 2023, available in German at: http://bit.ly/3ZZmEMf.\n999\n       MDR, Personalnot und Überforderung: Probleme in Erstaufnahmeeinrichtung nehmen zu, 29 November 2022,\n       available in German at: https://bit.ly/3GZS6B6.\n1000\n       Federal Government, response to information request by The Left, 20/8222, 05 September 2023, available in\n       German at: https://bit.ly/48E1rL3.\n1001\n       Terre des hommes, Zur Lebenssituation von minderjährigen Geflüchteten in Aufnahmeeinrichtungen, June\n       2020, available in German at: https://bit.ly/38Etah0, 7.\n1002\n       Bundesweite Arbeitsgemeinschaft der Psychosozialen Zentren für Flüchtlinge und Folteropfer (BAfF), Living\n       in a box - In order to be included in the monthly quota of 1,000 visa, ‘humanitarian\nreasons’ shall be decisive, which are listed in the law as follows:\n     v Long duration of separation of family members,\n     v Separation of families with at least one (minor) unmarried child,\n     v Serious risks to life, limb or personal freedom of a family member living abroad,\n     v Serious illness, need for care or serious disabilities of a family member living abroad.\n\nIn addition, the welfare of the child and ‘integration aspects’ (e.g., language skills, ability to provide for\nmeans of living) may be taken into account.1480\n\nThe monthly quota for visa has not been reached since the introduction of the new regulation for\nbeneficiaries of subsidiary protection, due to a complicated procedure involving three different authorities:\nEmbassies or consulates – often in cooperation with IOM – have to carry out an interview with the family\nmembers who have applied for visa; then the local alien’s offices in Germany have to decide whether the\nnecessary humanitarian criteria are fulfilled; and then they have to pass on the visa applications to the\nFederal Administrative Office (Bundesverwaltungsamt) which theoretically should select the most urgent\n1,000 cases per month.1481 In practice, this selection does not take place since procedures at the local\nauthorities are lengthy, resulting in less than 1,000 applications per month. As a result, the Federal\nAdministrative Office usually authorises all cases submitted by the local authorities and informs the\nembassies or consulates that visas may be issued.\n\nUntil 12 December 2023, 12,067 visas were issued to family members of beneficiaries of subsidiary\nprotection.1482 In contrast to the time between August 2018 and April 2021, where only 20,600 visas were\ngranted(62% of the total of 33,000 visas that the law would have foreseen for this period), the quota of\n12,000 per annum is thus fulfilled in 2023.1483 In 2022, 8,900 visas were issued, i.e., 74% of the quota of\n12,000 per year.1484 Around 17,594 requests for appointments at embassies were pending as of June\n2022.1485 Since it is likely that many persons have asked for appointments several times, the actual\nnumber of persons applying for visa for this purpose is likely to be lower.1486 10,778 of those visas in 2023\nwere granted to Syrian nationals, 176 to Afghan, 54 to Turkish, 83 to Iraqi and 2 to Iranian nationals.1487\n\nAlso for beneficiaries of subsidiary protection, the question arises concerning the time of their minority in\nrelation to family reunification. As already mentioned, the German Courts have in the past evaluated the\nage of the beneficiary at the time of the court decision and must now refer to the time of the asylum\napplication due to several CJEU rulings (see above). However, those rulings are based on the directive\n2003/86/EC,1488 which does not apply to beneficiaries of subsidiary protection.1489 Thus, German courts\nhave argued, that the right to family A trois, c’est encore plus compliqué. Les tensions qui traversent\nla coalition « feu tricolore »\nau pouvoir en Allemagne le confirment. Quinze mois après son élection, le chancelier social-démocrate Olaf Scholz (SPD) peine à donner un cap clair à sa politique, tiraillé entre les positions de son parti et celles de ses partenaires écologistes et libéraux (FDP), de plus en plus difficiles à concilier.\nSigne, le plus récent, de ces divergences : le report de la présentation en conseil des ministres du projet de budget pour 2024. Prévue mercredi 15 mars, elle a été différée sine die, faute d’accord entre le ministre des finances, Christian Lindner (FDP), qui se veut le garant vétilleux de l’équilibre des comptes publics, et ses collègues du SPD et des Verts, dont les exigences se révèlent particulièrement coûteuses.\nC’est le cas du ministre de la défense, Boris Pistorius (SPD), qui demande pour son prochain budget 10 milliards d’euros de plus que les 50 milliards d’euros prévus jusqu’à présent pour 2024. Sur le principe, Christian Lindner n’est pas contre une augmentation des dépenses militaires, mais à condition que d’autres ministres se serrent la ceinture.\nIl vous reste 76.12% de cet article à lire.
